G. Do the Check
SUBTASK 73-11-04-010-001-F00
(1) Do this task: Fuel Nozzle Filter Removal (SAC), TASK 73-11-03-000-802-F00.
SUBTASK 73-11-04-680-001-F00
(2) Do these steps to drain the fuel manifold:
(a) Put the 5-gallon (19-liter) fuel resistant container, STD-1054 below the fuel nozzle at the
6:00 O'clock position.
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO LOOSEN THE COUPLING NUT . USE ONE TO
HOLD THE FITTING, AND THE OTHER TO LOOSEN THE COUPLING
NUT . IF YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, DAMAGE TO THE
EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(b) Disconnect the manifold from the fuel nozzle at the 6:00 O'clock position:
1) Disconnect the shroud from the fuel nozzle at the 6:00 O'clock position.
a) Move the shroud to get access to the coupling nut on the fuel manifold.
2) Use two wrenches to disconnect the coupling nut from the fuel nozzle at the 6:00
O'clock position.
(c) Disconnect the manifold from the fuel nozzle at the 12:00 O'clock position:
1) Disconnect the shroud from the fuel nozzle at the 12:00 O'clock position.
a) Move the shroud to get access to the coupling nut on the fuel manifold.
2) Use two wrenches to disconnect the coupling nut from the fuel nozzle at the 12:00
O'clock position.
(d) Let the fuel drain into the container.
(e) Use your hand to connect the manifold coupling nut to the nipple on the two fuel nozzles
at the 6:00 and 12:00 O'clock positions.
73-11-04
(f) Do these steps to tighten the coupling nuts at the 6:00 O'clock and 12:00 O'clock
positions:
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO TIGHTEN THE COUPLING NUT . USE
ONE TO HOLD THE FITTING, AND THE OTHER TO TIGHTEN THE
COUPLING NUT . IF YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, DAMAGE
TO THE EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
1) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut to 140-150 pound-inches (16-17
Newton meters).
2) Use two wrenches to loosen the coupling nut.
3) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut to 140-150 pound-inches (16-17
Newton meters).
4) Use two wrenches to loosen the coupling nut.
5) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut to 140-150 pound-inches (16-17
Newton meters).
(g) Do not attach the shrouds to the fuel nozzle at this time.
SUBTASK 73-11-04-480-001-F00
(3) Connect 0-100 PSIG nitrogen source, STD-1115 to the fuel manifold fitting where the fuel
nozzle filter was removed.
SUBTASK 73-11-04-160-001-F00
(4) Do these steps to remove fuel that remains in the manifold:
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS NO OPEN FLAME OR OTHER SOURCE OF
IGNITION NEAR THE ENGINE. THE FUEL VAPORS CAN CATCH FIRE. IF
THERE IS A FIRE, IT CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND
DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT .
(a) Put 80 to 100 psig (551 to 689 kPa) of nitrogen through the fuel manifold.
(b) Decrease the pressure to zero.
H. Leak Check
SUBTASK 73-11-04-790-001-F00
(1) Do the Leak Check of the Fuel Nozzle:
(a) Disconnect the shrouds from all of the fuel nozzles.
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS NO OPEN FLAME OR OTHER SOURCE OF
IGNITION NEAR THE ENGINE. THE FUEL VAPORS CAN CATCH FIRE. IF
THERE IS A FIRE, IT CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND
DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT .
(b) Put 10 psig (69 kPa) of nitrogen into the fuel manifold.
1) Make sure that the check valves in the fuel nozzles do not open at this pressure.
2) Slowly increase the pressure to 15 psig (104 kPa).
3) Make sure that the check valves in the fuel nozzles do not open as the pressure
increases.
a) If a check valve opens, then replace the fuel nozzle.
These are the tasks:
73-11-04
Fuel Nozzle Removal, TASK 73-11-04-000-805-F01 or Fuel Nozzle Removal,
TASK 73-11-04-000-804-F02,
Fuel Nozzle Installation, TASK 73-11-04-400-805-F01 or Fuel Nozzle
Installation, TASK 73-11-04-400-804-F02.
(c) Slowly increase the pressure until one of the check valves in the fuel nozzles opens.
1) Make sure that a check valve opens before you reach 60 psig (414 kPa).
NOTE: A check valve will usually open before you reach 30 psig (207 kPa).
(d) Slowly decrease the pressure until the check valves in the fuel nozzles are closed.
(e) Use Snoop Leak Detector compound, G00091 to look for leaks at the fuel nozzle
coupling nuts and the fuel manifold.
1) If you find a leak, then continue.
2) If you do not find a leak, then do the steps below to Put the Airplane in a
Serviceable Condition.
SUBTASK 73-11-04-900-001-F00
(2) If you find a leak in the tubes of the fuel manifold, then replace the engine.
These are the tasks:
Power Plant - Removal, TASK 71-00-02-000-801-F00,
Power Plant - Installation, TASK 71-00-02-400-801-F00.
SUBTASK 73-11-04-200-001-F00
(3) If you find a leak at the fuel nozzle coupling nuts, then do these steps:
(a) Use two wrenches to disconnect the fuel nozzle coupling nut.
(b) Visually examine the ferrule and seat of the coupling nut and the seat of the fuel nozzle.
1) If the ferrule and seat of the coupling nut is damaged, then replace the engine.
These are the tasks:
Power Plant - Removal, TASK 71-00-02-000-801-F00,
Power Plant - Installation, TASK 71-00-02-400-801-F00.
2) If the seat of the fuel nozzle is damaged, then replace the fuel nozzle.
These are the tasks:
Fuel Nozzle Removal, TASK 73-11-04-000-805-F01 or Fuel Nozzle Removal,
TASK 73-11-04-000-804-F02,
Fuel Nozzle Installation, TASK 73-11-04-400-805-F01 or Fuel Nozzle Installation,
TASK 73-11-04-400-804-F02.
(c) If no problem is found, then use your hand to connect the manifold coupling nut to the
fuel nozzle.
1) Do these steps to tighten the coupling nut:
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO TIGHTEN THE COUPLING NUT . USE
ONE TO HOLD THE FITTING, AND THE OTHER TO TIGHTEN
THE COUPLING NUT . IF YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES,
DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
a) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut to 140-150 pound-inches (16-17
Newton meters).
73-11-04
b) Use two wrenches to loosen the coupling nut.
c) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut to 140-150 pound-inches (16-17
Newton meters).
d) Use two wrenches to loosen the coupling nut.
e) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut to 140-150 pound-inches (16-17
Newton meters).
(d) Do the Leak Check (above) again.
1) If you find a leak, then replace the applicable fuel nozzle
(TASK 73-11-04-000-805-F01 or TASK 73-11-04-000-804-F02 and
TASK 73-11-04-400-805-F01 or TASK 73-11-04-400-804-F02).
2) If you do not find a leak, then do the steps below to Put the Airplane in a
Serviceable Condition.
I. Put the Airplane in a Serviceable Condition

TO THE EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.

4) Use two wrenches to loosen the coupling nut.
5) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut to 140-150 pound-inches (16-17
Newton meters).
SUBTASK 73-11-04-410-001-F00
(2) Attach the shrouds to the fuel nozzles:
(a) Apply vaseline, D00672 [CP5070] on the preformed packings.

SUBTASK 73-11-04-080-001-F00

SUBTASK 73-11-05-840-001-F00

(1) Do this task: Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-801-F00.
SUBTASK 73-11-05-900-001-F00
(2) If you find damage to the fuel manifold that is not in the limits, then replace the engine, unless
you are given other instructions.
NOTE: The fuel manifold is not a Line Replaceable Unit (LRU). To remove the fuel manifold,
you must disassemble the engine.
(a) These are the tasks:
Power Plant - Removal, TASK 71-00-02-000-801-F00,
Power Plant - Installation, TASK 71-00-02-400-801-F00.
SUBTASK 73-11-05-210-001-F00
(3) Visually examine the fuel manifold tubes:
73-11-05
(a) Cracks, splits, or kinks in the manifold tubes are not acceptable.
(b) Wrinkles in the bends of the manifold tubes are acceptable with this limit:
1) The depth of the wrinkle must be less than 3 percent of the outside diameter of the
tube.
(c) Dents in the manifold are acceptable with these limits:
1) Not more than one dent in one linear foot (0.305 M) of the tube.
2) The depth of the dent must be less than 20 percent of the outside diameter of the
tube.
3) The minimum radius of the dent must be greater than 0.070 inch (1.78 mm).
(d) Nicks, scratches, scores, and chafed areas are acceptable with this limit:
1) The depth of the nick, scratch, score, and chafed area must be less than 0.005 inch
(0.13 mm) in depth after the tube is blended to a smooth radius.
SUBTASK 73-11-05-210-002-F00
(4) Examine the fuel nozzle supply tubes:
(a) Cracks, splits, or kinks in the nozzle supply tubes are not acceptable.
(b) Wrinkles in the bends of the nozzle supply tubes are acceptable with these limits:
1) The depth of the wrinkle must be less than 3 percent of the outside diameter of the
tube.
(c) Dents in the nozzle supply tubes are acceptable with these limits:
1) Not more than one dent in each of the tubes.
2) The depth of the dent must be less than 20 percent of the outside diameter of the
tube.
3) The minimum radius of the dent must be greater than 0.070 inch (1.78 mm).
(d) Flattened areas in the nozzle supply tubes are acceptable with these limits:
1) The length of the flattened area must be less than 2 times the outside diameter of
the tube.
2) The flattened area must change the contour of the tube by less than 10 percent of
its outside diameter.
(e) Nicks, scratches, scores, and chafed areas are acceptable with this limit:
1) The depth of the nick, scratch, score, and chafed area must be less than 0.005 inch
(0.13 mm) in depth after the tube is blended to a smooth radius.
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH SINGLE ANNULAR COMBUSTOR (SAC) ENGINES
2) For fuel nozzle supply tube number 7, the maximum limit of depth for nick, scratch,
score, and chafed area is 0.018 inch (0.45 mm) after removal of high metal.
a) Make sure that there is minimum clearance of 0.125 inch (3.17 mm) with the
bolt of the fire detector harness bracket (TASK 26-11-02-400-802).
SUBTASK 73-11-05-210-004-F00
NOTE: Do this inspection if the fuel manifold shroud coupling nut has been disconnected from its

(a) Damage must be less than one-half of a thread, cumulative or continuous.

SUBTASK 73-11-05-900-005-F00
(6) If you find damage to the shroud coupling nut thread that is not in limits, then replace the
shroud coupling nut. These are the steps:
(a) Remove the fuel nozzle that mates with the damaged shroud coupling nut. Do this task,
Fuel Nozzle Removal, TASK 73-11-04-000-805-F01 or Fuel Nozzle Removal,
TASK 73-11-04-000-804-F02.
(b) With applicable tool, remove the retaining ring from the shroud.
(c) Slide the shroud aft and remove it from the fuel manifold tube.
(d) Slide the shroud coupling nut off the shroud. If the shroud coupling nut does not remove
easily from the shroud, replace the shroud coupling nut and the shroud.
(e) Install the shroud coupling nut on the shroud. Make sure that the open portion of the nut
will face aft when the shroud is installed on the fuel manifold tube.
(f) Install the shroud on the fuel manifold tube. Make sure that the open portion of the
shroud faces aft after installation.
(g) With the applicable tool, install the retaining ring in the shroud. After installation, apply
pressure to the retaining ring to make sure it is retained in the groove in the shroud.
(h) Install the fuel nozzle, do this task Fuel Nozzle Installation, TASK 73-11-04-400-805-F01
or Fuel Nozzle Installation, TASK 73-11-04-400-804-F02.
RYR ALL

(a) If not already done, disconnect the fuel supply tube from the applicable fuel nozzle
(TASK 73-11-04-000-805-F01 or TASK 73-11-04-000-804-F02).
(b) Axial scratches in Areas A and C are acceptable after the surface is blended smooth.
(c) Axial scratches in Area B are not acceptable.
(d) Circumferential scratches in Areas A and C are acceptable after the surface is blended
smooth.
73-11-05
(e) Circumferential scratches in Area B are acceptable with these limits:
1) The surface is blended smooth.
2) The scratch is too small to measure with a 0.040 inch (1.02 mm) radius ball scriber.
(f) Nicks and pits in Areas A, B, and C are not acceptable.
(g) Damaged threads are acceptable with these limits:
1) Use a tap or a die to chase the damaged threads.
2) After you chase the threads, the damage must be less than one-half of a thread,
cumulative or continuous.

2. IDG Oil Cooler Removal
(Figure 401)
A. General
(1) The engine has one IDG oil cooler.
(2) The IDG oil cooler is on the fan frame, aft of the lubrication unit, at the 7:00 o'clock position.
B. References
Reference Title
24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201)
24-22-00-860-812 Remove Electrical Power (P/B 201)
70-30-01-910-802-F00 Seals (Preformed Packings and O-Rings) and Gaskets
(P/B 201)

C. Tools/Equipment
Reference Description
STD-858 Tag - DO NOT OPERATE

D. Location Zones
Zone Area
411 Engine 1 - Engine
421 Engine 2 - Engine
E. Prepare for the Removal
SUBTASK 73-11-06-840-001-F00
(1) Prepare for the procedure as follows:
(a) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811.
(b) Make sure that the engine start lever is in the CUTOFF position.
1) Install a DO NOT OPERATE tag, STD-858, on the applicable engine start lever.
(c) Make sure that the ENG VALVE CLOSED (engine fuel shutoff valve) light on the fuel
control panel (P5 overhead panel) is dim.
NOTE: The light for the engine fuel shutoff valve has three positions: 1) bright when the
valve is in transition or does not agree with the commanded position; 2) dim
when the valve is closed; or 3) off when the valve is opened.
73-11-06
(d) Make sure that the SPAR VALVE CLOSED light on the fuel control panel (P5 overhead
panel) is dim.
NOTE: The light for the spar shutoff valve has three positions: 1) bright when the valve is
in transition or does not agree with the commanded position; 2) dim when the
valve is closed; or 3) off when the valve is opened.
(e) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812.
1) Make sure that the BAT switch on P5-13 panel is set to OFF and install a DO NOT
OPERATE tag, STD-858.
(f) Do this task: Open the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-010-801-F00.
F . IDG Oil Cooler Removal
SUBTASK 73-11-06-020-002-F00
(1) Open the 1/4-turn Omega clamps [10].
(a) Remove the J5, J6, J7, and J8 wire harnesses from the Omega clamps [10].
(b) Move the wire harnesses away from the IDG oil cooler [5].
1) If it is necessary, use string or tape to hold the wires out of your way.
SUBTASK 73-11-06-680-001-F00
WARNING
DO NOT GET FUEL IN YOUR MOUTH, OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. DO
NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE FUEL. KEEP THE FUEL AWAY FROM
SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . FUEL IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE,
WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE COMPONENTS OF THE OIL SYSTEM IF THE ENGINE
IS HOT . THESE COMPONENTS STAY HOTTER THAN OTHER
COMPONENTS. HOT COMPONENTS CAN BURN YOU.
WARNING
DO NOT OPEN THE OIL SYSTEM UNTIL THE PRESSURE GOES TO ZERO.
THE PRESSURE GOES TO ZERO APPROXIMATELY 5 MINUTES AFTER AN
ENGINE IS STOPPED. A PRESSURIZED OIL SYSTEM CAN RELEASE A
SPRAY OF HOT OIL THAT CAN BURN YOU.
WARNING
DO NOT LET HOT OIL GET ON YOU. PUT ON CLOTHES, GOGGLES, AND
OTHER EQUIPMENT FOR PROTECTION, OR LET THE ENGINE BECOME
COOL. HOT OIL CAN BURN YOU.
CAUTION
DO NOT LET HOT OIL GET ON THE ENGINE OR OTHER COMPONENTS.
IMMEDIATELY CLEAN THE COMPONENT IF OIL FALLS ON IT . OIL CAN
CAUSE DAMAGE TO PAINT AND RUBBER.
(2) Drain the IDG oil cooler [5] as follows:

E. Prepare for the Installation
SUBTASK 73-11-06-840-002-F00
(1) Prepare for the procedure as follows:
(a) Remove the protective covers from the Integrated Drive Generator (IDG) oil cooler [5]
and its oil and fuel tubes.
(b) Make sure that the mating surfaces of the IDG oil cooler [5] are clean.
(c) Examine all of the component mating surfaces and the adjacent areas.
1) Make sure that the mating surfaces are clean.
2) Make sure that the components are not damaged.
(d) Re-install the protective covers on the IDG oil cooler [5] and its oil and fuel tubes.
SUBTASK 73-11-06-420-002-F00
(2) Install the drain plug [20] into the IDG oil cooler [5]:
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB
POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN.
(a) Lubricate the new O-ring [15] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
(b) Install the new O-ring [15] on the drain plug [20].
(c) Lubricate the threads of the drain plug [20] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
(d) Install the drain plug [20].
1) Tighten the drain plug [20] to a torque of 45 in-lb (5.08 N·m) - 55 in-lb (6.21 N·m).
(e) Install the safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on the drain
plug [20].
73-11-06
SUBTASK 73-11-06-020-008-F00

oil ports on the IDG oil cooler [5].
(b) Lubricate the threads of bolts [130] and bolts [145] with graphite mineral vaseline,
D00601 [CP2101].
(c) Put the oil-out nipple [120] in the correct position on the IDG oil cooler [5].
1) Install the bolts [130] to attach the oil-out nipple [120] to the IDG oil cooler [5].
2) Tighten the bolts [130] to 49 in-lb (5.54 N·m) - 53 in-lb (5.99 N·m).
(d) Put the oil-in nipple [135] in the correct position on the IDG oil cooler [5].
1) Install the bolts [145] to attach the oil-in nipple [135] to the IDG oil cooler [5].
2) Tighten the bolts [145] to 49 in-lb (5.54 N·m) - 53 in-lb (5.99 N·m).

2. Servo Fuel Heater Removal
(Figure 401)
A. General
(1) The servo fuel heater is on the engine, below the left fan cowl panel, at the 8:00 o'clock
position.
(2) The servo fuel heater is installed on the top of the fuel pump package, adjacent to the main
oil/fuel heat exchanger.
(3) The servo fuel heater is attached to the Hydro-Mechanical Unit (HMU) and main oil/fuel heat
exchanger.

SUBTASK 73-11-07-840-001-F00
(1) Do these steps to prepare the airplane for the removal:
(a) Make sure that the applicable start lever is in the CUTOFF position.
1) Install a DO NOT OPERATE tag, STD-858, on the applicable start lever.
73-11-07
(b) Make sure that the ENG VALVE CLOSED (engine fuel shutoff valve) light, on the P5-2
fuel control panel, is dim.
NOTE: The light for the engine fuel shutoff valve has three positions:
- Bright when the valve is in transition or does not agree with the commanded
position
- Dim when the valve is closed
- Off when the valve is opened.
(c) Make sure that the SPAR VALVE CLOSED light, on the P5-2 fuel control panel, is dim.
NOTE: The light for the spar shutoff valve has three positions:
- Bright when the valve is in transition or does not agree with the commanded
position
- Dim when the valve is closed
- Off when the valve is opened.
(d) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812.
1) Make sure that the BAT switch, on the P5-13 panel, is set to the OFF position.
a) Install a DO NOT OPERATE tag, STD-858, on the BAT switch.

H. Servo Fuel Heater Removal
SUBTASK 73-11-07-680-001-F00
(1) Do these steps to drain the fuel from the fuel pump [5]:
WARNING
DO NOT GET FUEL IN YOUR MOUTH, EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. DO
NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE FUEL. PUT ON GOGGLES, AND
GLOVES WHEN YOU USE FUEL. KEEP FUEL AWAY FROM SPARKS,
FLAME, AND HEAT . FUEL IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE. FUEL CAN
CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Put a 5-gallon (19-liter) fuel resistant container, STD-1054, below the fuel pump.
(b) Remove the safety wire from the drain plug [3].
(c) Remove the drain plug [3] from the fuel filter cover.
(d) Let the fuel drain in the container.
(e) Remove and discard the O-ring [4] from the drain plug [3].
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB
POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN.
(f) Lubricate a new O-ring [4] with oil, D00623 [CP5066], or vaseline, D00672 [CP5070].
(g) Install a O-ring [4] on the drain plug [3].
(h) Lubricate the threads of the drain plug [3] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
(i) Install the drain plug [3].
1) Tighten the drain plug [3] to 45 in-lb (5.1 N·m) - 55 in-lb (6.2 N·m).
(j) Install safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on the drain
plug [3] (TASK 70-20-01-800-805-F00).
73-11-07
SUBTASK 73-11-07-210-001-F00
(2) Make sure that the container stays below the fuel pump during the removal procedure.
(a) As you disassemble the servo-fuel heater [1], let the unwanted fuel and oil drain into the
container.
SUBTASK 73-11-07-020-001-F00
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE COMPONENTS OF THE OIL SYSTEM IF THE ENGINE
IS HOT . THESE COMPONENTS STAY HOTTER THAN OTHER
COMPONENTS. HOT COMPONENTS CAN BURN YOU.
WARNING
DO NOT OPEN THE OIL SYSTEM UNTIL THE PRESSURE GOES TO ZERO.
THE PRESSURE GOES TO ZERO APPROXIMATELY 5 MINUTES AFTER AN
ENGINE IS STOPPED. A PRESSURIZED OIL SYSTEM CAN RELEASE A
SPRAY OF HOT OIL THAT CAN BURN YOU.
WARNING
DO NOT LET HOT OIL GET ON YOU. PUT ON CLOTHES, GOGGLES, AND
OTHER EQUIPMENT FOR PROTECTION, OR LET THE ENGINE BECOME
COOL. HOT OIL CAN BURN YOU.
WARNING
DO NOT GET ENGINE OIL ON YOUR SKIN FOR A LONG TIME. FLUSH THE
OIL FROM YOUR SKIN WITH WATER. THE OIL IS POISONOUS AND CAN GO
THROUGH YOUR SKIN AND IN TO YOUR BODY .
CAUTION
DO NOT LET HOT OIL GET ON THE ENGINE OR OTHER COMPONENTS.
IMMEDIATELY CLEAN THE COMPONENT IF OIL FALLS ON IT . OIL CAN
CAUSE DAMAGE TO PAINT AND RUBBER.
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO LOOSEN THE COUPLING NUT . USE ONE TO
HOLD THE FITTING, AND THE OTHER TO LOOSEN THE COUPLING NUT . IF
YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, YOU CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE
EQUIPMENT .
(3) Do these steps to remove the oil tube [11] from the servo-fuel heater [1]:
(a) Remove the nut [15], bolt [16], and clamps [17] that hold the oil tube [11] on the bracket.
NOTE: The nut and bolt also hold a clamp for the fuel tube.
1) Let the other clamp stay on the fuel tube [14].
NOTE: The clamp can be removed from the fuel tube and kept for installation.
(b) Remove the nut [18], bolt [19], and clamp [20] that hold the oil tube [11] on the bracket.
(c) Use two wrenches to disconnect the oil tube [11] from the oil tube [10].
(d) Remove the four bolts [13] that hold the oil tube [11] and gasket [12] on the servo-fuel
heater [1].
1) If the gasket [12] is serviceable, keep it with the tube for the subsequent installation
(TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
2) Remove the oil tube [11].
73-11-07
a) Install protective covers on the oil tube [11], oil tube [10], and servo-fuel
heater [1].
SUBTASK 73-11-07-010-001-F00
(4) Do these steps to remove the fuel tube [21] from the HMU [2]:
(a) Remove the nut [18], bolt [19], and clamp [20] that hold the fuel tube [21] on the bracket.
(b) Use two wrenches to disconnect the fuel tube [21] from the fuel tube [22].
(c) Remove the four bolts [24] that hold the fuel tube [21] and gasket [23] on the HMU [2].
1) If the gasket [23] is serviceable, keep it with the tube for the subsequent installation
(TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
2) Remove the fuel tube [21].
a) Install protective covers on the fuel tube [21], fuel tube [22], and HMU [2].
SUBTASK 73-11-07-020-002-F00
(5) Do these steps to disconnect the oil tube [27] from the servo-fuel heater [1]:
(a) Remove the four bolts [26] that hold the oil tube [27] and gasket [25] on the servo-fuel
heater [1].
1) Move the oil tube [27] out of the way, to make sure that it is not damaged.
a) If it is necessary, use string or tape to keep the tube out of the way.
2) If the gasket [25] is serviceable, keep it with the tube for the subsequent installation
(TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
a) Install protective covers on the oil tube [27] and servo-fuel heater [1].
SUBTASK 73-11-07-020-003-F00
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO LOOSEN THE COUPLING NUT . USE ONE TO
HOLD THE FITTING, AND THE OTHER TO LOOSEN THE COUPLING NUT . IF
YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, YOU CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE
EQUIPMENT .
(6) Do these steps to remove the fuel tube [30] and fuel tube [31] from the servo-fuel heater [1]:
(a) Use two wrenches to disconnect the fuel tube [30] from the servo-fuel heater [1] and fuel
pump [5].
1) Remove the fuel tube [30].
2) Install protective covers on the fuel tube [30], servo-fuel heater [1], and fuel
pump [5].
(b) Use two wrenches to disconnect the fuel tube [31] from the servo-fuel heater [1].
(c) Remove the four bolts [32] that hold the fuel tube [31] and gasket [33] on the HMU [2].
1) Remove the fuel tube [31].
a) If the gasket [33] is serviceable, keep it with the tube for the subsequent
installation (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
2) Install protective covers on the fuel tube [31], servo-fuel heater [1], and HMU [2].
SUBTASK 73-11-07-020-004-F00
(7) Do these steps to remove the servo-fuel heater [1]:
(a) Remove the nut [45], bolt [46], and clamp [47] that hold the fuel tube [14] on the
bracket [44].
73-11-07
(b) Remove the three nuts [42] that hold the servo-fuel heater [1] and bracket [44] on the
outboard side of the main oil/fuel heat exchanger [40].
(c) Remove the three nuts [42] and washer [43] that hold the servo-fuel heater [1] on the
inboard side of the main oil/fuel heat exchanger [40].
(d) Carefully remove the servo-fuel heater [1], bracket [44], and gasket [41] from the main
oil/fuel heat exchanger [40].
1) If the gasket [41] is serviceable, keep it with the servo-fuel heater [1] for the
subsequent installation (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
2) Remove the nipple [48] and nipple [50].
a) Remove and discard the O-rings [49] from the nipple [48] and nipple [50].
3) Install protective covers on the main oil/fuel heat exchanger [40] and servo-fuel
heater [1].

E. Servo Fuel Heater Installation
SUBTASK 73-11-07-020-005-F00
(1) Install the servo-fuel heater [1] on the main oil/fuel heat exchanger [40].
(a) Remove the protective covers from the mating surfaces of the main oil/fuel heat
exchanger [40] and the servo-fuel heater [1].
(b) Lubricate the applicable parts:
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB
POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN.
1) Lubricate the gasket [41] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
2) Lubricate the threads of the studs on the main oil/fuel heat exchanger [40] with
graphite mineral vaseline, D00601 [CP2101].
(c) Put the gasket [41], servo-fuel heater [1], and the bracket [44] in the correct position on
the main oil/fuel heat exchanger [40].
(d) Install the three nuts [42] that hold the servo-fuel heater [1] and the bracket [44] on the
outboard side of the main oil/fuel heat exchanger [40].
(e) Install the three nuts [42] and the three washers [43] that hold the servo-fuel heater [1] on
the inboard side of the main oil/fuel heat exchanger [40].
1) Tighten the six nuts [42] to 98 in-lb (11 N·m) - 110 in-lb (12 N·m) in a criss-cross
pattern.
(f) Install the nut [45], bolt [46], and the clamp [47] that hold the fuel tube [14] on the
bracket [44].
1) Tighten the nut [45] to 98 in-lb (11 N·m) - 110 in-lb (12 N·m).
SUBTASK 73-11-07-420-001-F00
(2) Do these steps to install the nipple [48] and nipple [50]:
73-11-07
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB
POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN.
(a) Lubricate two new O-rings [49] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
(b) Install one O-ring [49] in the groove of nipple [48] and nipple [50].
(c) Lubricate the threads of the nipple [48] and nipple [50] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
(d) Install the nipple [48] and nipple [50] in the servo fuel-heater ports.
(e) Tighten the nipple [48] and nipple [50] to 361 in-lb (41 N·m) - 399 in-lb (45 N·m).
(f) Install safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], between the
nipple [48] and nipple [50].
SUBTASK 73-11-07-020-006-F00
CAUTION
DO NOT APPLY TOO MUCH BENDING LOAD ON THE FUEL TUBE WHEN
YOU INSTALL IT . TOO MUCH BENDING CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE PART .
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO TORQUE THE COUPLING NUT . USE ONE TO
HOLD THE FITTING, AND THE OTHER TO TURN THE COUPLING NUT . IF
YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT CAN
OCCUR.
(3) Do these steps to install the fuel tube [31] on the servo-fuel heater [1]:
(a) Remove the protective covers from the fuel tube [31].
(b) Remove the applicable protective covers from the servo-fuel heater [1] and HMU [2].
(c) Lubricate the applicable parts:
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB
POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN.
1) Lubricate the gasket [33] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
2) Lubricate the threads of the four bolts [32] with graphite mineral vaseline, D00601
[CP2101].
3) Lubricate the threads of the nipple [48] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
(d) Put the fuel tube [31] and the gasket [33] in the correct position between the servo-fuel
heater [1] and the HMU [2].
(e) Use your hand to connect the fuel tube [31] to the servo-fuel heater [1].
(f) Install the four bolts [32] that hold the fuel tube [31] and the gasket [33] on the HMU [2].
1) Tighten the four bolts [32] to 49 in-lb (6 N·m) - 53 in-lb (6 N·m).
(g) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut between the fuel tube [31] and the
servo-fuel heater [1] to 650 in-lb (73 N·m) - 770 in-lb (87 N·m).
73-11-07
SUBTASK 73-11-07-020-007-F00
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS A CLEARANCE BETWEEN THE OIL TUBE AND
ADJACENT PARTS AFTER INSTALLATION. THIS WILL PREVENT DAMAGE
ON FAN FRAME SHROUD SKIN.
(4) Do these steps to connect the oil tube [27] to the servo-fuel heater [1]:
(a) Remove the protective cover from the oil tube [27].
(b) Remove the applicable protective cover from the servo-fuel heater [1].
(c) Lubricate the applicable parts:
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB
POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN.
1) Lubricate the gasket [25] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
2) Lubricate the threads of the four bolts [26] with graphite mineral vaseline, D00601
[CP2101].
(d) Put the oil tube [27] and the gasket [25] in the correct position on the servo-fuel
heater [1].
(e) Install the four bolts [26] that hold the oil tube [27] and the gasket [25] on the servo-fuel
heater [1].
1) Tighten the four bolts [26] to 49 in-lb (6 N·m) - 53 in-lb (6 N·m).
SUBTASK 73-11-07-410-001-F00
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU USE APPROVED BOLTS WHEN YOU CONNECT THE
LINES TO THE HMU. INSTALLATION OF INCORRECT ATTACHMENT BOLTS
CAN CAUSE AN IN-FLIGHT FUEL LEAK.
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO TORQUE THE COUPLING NUT . USE ONE TO
HOLD THE FITTING, AND THE OTHER TO TURN THE COUPLING NUT . IF
YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT CAN
OCCUR.
(5) Do these steps to install the fuel tube [21] on the HMU [2] and on the fuel tube [22]:
NOTE: When you connect the lines to the Hydro-Mechanical Unit (HMU) and fuel spills out of
the lines, bubbles may occur after installation. Bubbles are acceptable provided the
bubbles stop after three cycles.
(a) Remove the protective covers from the fuel tube [21], fuel tube [22], and the HMU [2].
(b) Lubricate the applicable parts:
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. THE SYNTHETIC OIL
CONTAINS ADDITIVES THAT CAN BE POISONOUS IF THEY ARE
ABSORBED THROUGH THE SKIN. CLEAN AWAY ALL OIL THAT
GETS ON THE SKIN.
1) Lubricate the threads of the bolts [19] and the bolts [24] with graphite mineral
vaseline, D00601 [CP2101].
73-11-07
2) Lubricate the threads of the fuel tube [21] and the fuel tube [22] with oil, D00623
[CP5066].
3) Lubricate the gasket [23] with vaseline, D00672 [CP5070].
4) Lubricate the threads of the nipple with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
CAUTION
LOOK AT THE GASKET BEFORE YOU INSTALL IT . OBEY THE
INSTRUCTIONS IN SEALS (PREFORMED PACKINGS AND O-RINGS)
AND GASKETS, TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00. DO NOT CAUSE DAMAGE
TO THE RUBBER SEAL WHEN YOU INSTALL THE GASKET .
(c) Put the fuel tube [21] and the gasket [23] in the correct position between the fuel tube [22]
and the HMU [2].
(d) Use your hand to install the four bolts [24] that hold the fuel tube [21] and the gasket [23]
on the HMU [2].
(e) Use your hands to connect the fuel tube [21] to the fuel tube [22].
(f) Make sure that the fuel tube [21] is in its correct position.
1) Make sure that there is no gap between the fuel tube [21] and the gasket [23] or
between the gasket [23] and the HMU [2].
(g) Use your hands to install the nut [18], bolt [19], and clamp [20] that hold the fuel tube [21]
on the bracket.
CAUTION
TIGHTEN THE BOLTS CORRECTLY . REFER TO TIGHTENING
PRACTICES AND TORQUE VALUES, TASK 70-20-02-400-801-F00. IF
YOU TIGHTEN THEM INCORRECTLY , DAMAGE TO THE PARTS CAN
OCCUR.
(h) Tighten the four bolts [24] to 97 in-lb (11 N·m) - 110 in-lb (12 N·m).
(i) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut between the fuel tube [21] and the fuel
tube [22] to 900 in-lb (102 N·m) - 1100 in-lb (124 N·m).
(j) Tighten the two nuts [18] for the clamps [20] to 98 in-lb (11 N·m) - 110 in-lb (12 N·m).
SUBTASK 73-11-07-020-009-F00
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO TORQUE THE COUPLING NUT . USE ONE TO
HOLD THE FITTING, AND THE OTHER TO TURN THE COUPLING NUT . IF
YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT CAN
OCCUR.
(6) Do these steps to install the fuel tube [30] on the servo-fuel heater [1]:
(a) Remove the protective covers from the fuel tube [30].
(b) Remove the applicable protective covers from the servo-fuel heater [1] and fuel pump [5].
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. THE SYNTHETIC OIL CONTAINS
ADDITIVES THAT CAN BE POISONOUS IF THEY ARE ABSORBED
THROUGH THE SKIN. CLEAN AWAY ALL OIL THAT GETS ON THE SKIN.
(c) Lubricate the threads of the nipple [50] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
(d) Put the fuel tube [30] in the correct position between the servo-fuel heater [1] and the fuel
pump [5].
73-11-07
(e) Use your hands to connect the fuel tube [30] to the servo-fuel heater [1] and the fuel
pump [5].
(f) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut between the fuel tube [30] and the fuel
pump [5] to 650 in-lb (73 N·m) - 770 in-lb (87 N·m).
(g) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut between the fuel tube [30] and the
servo-fuel heater [1] to 650 in-lb (73 N·m) - 770 in-lb (87 N·m).
SUBTASK 73-11-07-020-008-F00
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO TORQUE THE COUPLING NUT . USE ONE TO
HOLD THE FITTING, AND THE OTHER TO TURN THE COUPLING NUT . IF
YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT CAN
OCCUR.
(7) Do these steps to install the oil tube [11] on the servo-fuel heater [1]:
(a) Remove the protective covers from the oil tube [11], oil tube [10], and the servo-fuel
heater [1].
(b) Lubricate the applicable parts:
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB
POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN.
1) Lubricate the gasket [12] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
2) Lubricate the threads of the bolts [13], bolts [16], and the bolts [19] with graphite
mineral vaseline, D00601 [CP2101].
3) Lubricate the threads of the nipple of the oil tube [10] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
4) Lubricate the threads of the oil tube [10] and the oil tube [11] with oil, D00623
[CP5066].
(c) Put the oil tube [11] and the gasket [12] in the correct position between the oil tube [10]
and the servo-fuel heater [1].
(d) Loosely connect the oil tube [11] to the oil tube [10].
(e) Install the four bolts [13] that hold the oil tube [11] and the gasket [12] on the servo-fuel
heater [1].
(f) Make sure that the clamp [20] on the oil tube [10] aligns with the bracket [44].
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT THE CLAMPS DO NOT INTERFERE WITH THE HMU,
THE FUEL TUBES OR THE OIL TUBES. IF INTERFERENCE OCCURS, IT
CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT .
(g) Install the nut [15], bolt [16], and the clamps [17] that hold the oil tube [11] and the fuel
tube [14].
(h) Install the nut [18], bolt [19], and the clamp [20] that hold the oil tube [11] on the
bracket [44].
(i) Tighten the four bolts [13] to 49 in-lb (6 N·m) - 53 in-lb (6 N·m).
(j) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut between the oil tube [11] and the oil
tube [10] to 900 in-lb (102 N·m) - 1000 in-lb (113 N·m).
73-11-07
(k) Make sure that the oil tube [11] is in its correct position and does not touch other parts of
the engine.
(l) Tighten the nut [15] and nut [18] to 98 in-lb (11 N·m) - 110 in-lb (12 N·m).
F . Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 73-11-07-840-002-F00
(1) Do these steps to put the airplane in a serviceable condition:
(a) Do this task: Close the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-410-801-F00.
(b) Remove the DO NOT OPERATE tag from the applicable start lever.
(c) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811.
1) Remove the DO NOT OPERATE tag from the BAT switch, on the P5-13 panel.

(TASK 71-00-00-800-811-F00).

73-11-08-000-801-F00Burner Staging Valve RemovalPDF p. 253

2. Burner Staging Valve Removal
(Figure 401)
A. General
(1) The burner staging valve is found on the lower side of engine, forward of the fuel nozzles.
(2) For this procedure the burner staging valve will be referred to as the BSV.
RYR ALL POST SB CFM56-7B-73-054 AND POST SB CFM56-7B 73-044
(3) Engines with CFMI SB 73-044 do not have the Burner Staging Valve installed.
RYR ALL POST SB CFM56-7B-73-054
(4) Engines with CFMI SB 73-054 do not have the Burner Staging Valve installed. The Burner
Staging Valve is replaced by a fuel cover.
RYR ALL

F . Burner Staging Valve Removal (BSV)
SUBTASK 73-11-08-020-001-F00
(1) Do these steps to remove the BSV [4] from the engine:
(a) Disconnect the electrical connectors DP0906 [1] and DP1006 [5] from the BSV [4].
(b) Put a 5-gallon (19-liter) fuel resistant container, STD-1054below the BSV [4].
WARNING
DO NOT GET FUEL IN YOUR MOUTH, OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN.
DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE FUEL. KEEP THE FUEL
AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . FUEL IS POISONOUS AND
FLAMMABLE, WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(c) Remove the four bolts [3] that hold the burner staging valve [4] to the fuel supply
manifold.
(d) Remove the BSV [4].
(e) Remove the gasket [2].
(f) Examine the gasket [2] (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
1) If the gasket [2] is damaged, discard the gasket.
2) If there is no damage, keep the gasket [2] for the subsequent installation.
(g) Install protective covers on the BSV [4] openings and the fuel supply manifold.

H. Fuel Supply Hose Removal
SUBTASK 73-11-10-680-001-F00
(1) Do these steps to drain the fuel from the fuel system:
WARNING
DO NOT GET FUEL IN YOUR MOUTH, EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. DO
NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE FUEL. PUT ON GOGGLES AND
GLOVES WHEN YOU DO FUEL SYSTEM MAINTENANCE. KEEP FUEL
AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . FUEL IS POISONOUS AND
FLAMMABLE. FUEL CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Put a 5-gallon (19-liter) fuel resistant container, STD-1054, below the fuel pump.
73-11-10
(b) Remove the drain plug [20] from the fuel filter cover.
(c) Let the fuel drain in the container.
(d) Remove and discard the packing [21] from the drain plug.
(e) Keep the container below the engine to catch the unwanted fuel that flows.
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB
POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN.
(f) Lubricate a new packing [21] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
(g) Install the new packing [21] on the drain plug [20].
(h) Lubricate the threads of the drain plug [20] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
(i) Install the drain plug [20].
1) Tighten the drain plug [20] to 45 in-lb (5.1 N·m) - 55 in-lb (6.2 N·m).
(j) Install safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on the drain
plug [20].
SUBTASK 73-11-10-020-001-F00
(2) Remove the bolts [3] and strap clamps [2] that hold the fuel supply hose [1] to the fan case.
(a) Keep the bolts [3] and strap clamps [2] for the subsequent installation.
SUBTASK 73-11-10-020-002-F00
(3) Do these steps to disconnect the fuel supply hose [1] from the engine fuel tube:
(a) Make sure that the container (5 gal.) is in a position to catch the remaining fuel that can
flow from the fuel supply hose [1].
(b) Remove the bolts [5] that hold the fuel supply hose [1] and the gasket [6] to the engine
fuel tube.
1) If the gasket [6] is serviceable, keep it with the tube for the subsequent installation
(TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
2) Keep the bolts [5] for the subsequent installation.
SUBTASK 73-11-10-020-004-F00
(4) Do these steps to disconnect the fuel supply hose [1] from the strut fitting:
(a) Use two wrenches to disconnect the B-nut from the strut fitting.
NOTE: One wrench to hold the fuel supply hose and one wrench to turn the B-nut.

E. Fuel Supply Hose Installation
SUBTASK 73-11-10-420-001-F00
(1) Install the fuel supply hose [1] in its correct position on the engine fan case:
(a) Do these steps to connect the fuel supply hose [1] to the strut fitting:
1) Use your hand to connect the B-nut to the strut fitting.
2) Use two wrenches to tighten the B-nut to 1140 in-lb (128.8 N·m) - 1260 in-lb
(142.4 N·m).
NOTE: One wrench to hold the fuel supply hose and one wrench to turn the B-nut.
3) Use two wrenches to loosen the B-nut fitting.
4) Use two wrenches to re-tighten the B-nut to a 1140 in-lb (128.8 N·m) to 1260 in-lb
(142.4 N·m).
(b) Lubricate the gasket [6] with vaseline, D00672 [CP5070].
(c) Put the fuel supply hose [1] and the gasket [6] in their correct position on the engine fuel
tube.
(d) Lubricate the threads of the bolts [5] with Pure Nickel Special compound, D00006.
(e) Use the bolts [5] to connect the fuel supply hose [1] and the gasket [6] to the engine fuel
tube.
1) Tighten the bolts [5] to 50 in-lb (5.6 N·m) - 55 in-lb (6.2 N·m).
(f) Use the strap clamps [2] and the bolts [3] to loosely connect the fuel supply hose [1] to
the fan case.
1) Adjust the hose to distribute the hose equally.
2) Tighten the bolts [3] to 98 in-lb (11.1 N·m) - 110 in-lb (12.4 N·m).

2. Fuel Pump Removal
(Figure 401, Figure 402, Figure 403, and Figure 404).
A. General
(1) This task gives the instructions on how to remove the fuel pump.
(2) The fuel pump is on the engine at the 8:00 o'clock position below the left fan cowl panel.
(3) The fuel pump package is made up of the fuel pump, Hydro-Mechanical Unit (HMU), fuel filter,
main oil/fuel heat exchanger, servo fuel heater, and fuel filter differential pressure switch.
(4) It is easier to complete the task if you remove and install the same size tube connectors at the
same time.

73-11-11

H. Fuel Pump Removal
SUBTASK 73-11-11-680-001-F00
(1) Do these steps to drain the fuel from the fuel pump [1].
WARNING
DO NOT GET FUEL IN YOUR MOUTH, OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN.
DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE FUEL. KEEP THE FUEL
AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . FUEL IS POISONOUS AND
FLAMMABLE, WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS OR
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Put a 5 gallon (19 liter) container, STD-1154, below the fuel pump [1].
(b) Cut and discard safety wire from the drain plug [2].
(c) Remove the drain plug [2] from the fuel filter cover.
(d) Let the fuel drain in the container.
(e) Remove and discard the O-ring [3] from the drain plug [2].
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB
POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN.
(f) Lubricate a new O-ring [3] with oil, D00623 [CP5066], or vaseline, D00672 [CP5070].
(g) Install the new O-ring [3] on the drain plug [2].
(h) Lubricate the threads of the drain plug [2] with oil, D00623 [CP5066].
(i) Install the drain plug [2].
1) Tighten the drain plug [2] to 45.0 in-lb (5.08 N·m) - 55.0 in-lb (6.21 N·m).
(j) Install the safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on the drain
plug [2].
SUBTASK 73-11-11-680-002-F00
(2) Make sure that the container stays below the fuel pump during the removal.
(a) While the fuel pump assembly is disassembled, let the unwanted fuel and oil drain into
the container.
SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-001-F00
(3) Do this task: HMU Removal, TASK 73-21-10-000-801-F00.
SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-002-F00
(4) Do this task: Handcranking Drive Cover Removal, TASK 72-63-01-000-801-F00.
73-11-11
SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-003-F00
(5) Do this task: Servo Fuel Heater Removal, TASK 73-11-07-000-801-F00.
SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-004-F00
(6) Do this task: Main Oil/Fuel Heat Exchanger Removal, TASK 79-21-02-000-801-F00.
SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-005-F00
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO LOOSEN THE TUBE COUPLING NUTS. USE ONE
WRENCH TO HOLD THE NIPPLE FITTING. USE THE OTHER WRENCH TO
LOOSEN THE COUPLING NUT . IF THE NIPPLE FITTING TURNS, DAMAGE
CAN OCCUR.
(7) Do these steps to disconnect the fuel tube [8] from the hose [4]:
(a) Use two wrenches to disconnect the fuel tube [8] from the hose [4].
1) If it is necessary, use lockwire or tape to keep the hose [4] out of the way.
(b) Remove the fuel tube [8].
(c) Install protective covers on the fuel tube [8] and hose [4].
SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-006-F00
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO LOOSEN THE TUBE COUPLING NUTS. USE ONE
WRENCH TO HOLD THE NIPPLE FITTING. USE THE OTHER WRENCH TO
LOOSEN THE COUPLING NUT . IF THE NIPPLE FITTING TURNS, DAMAGE
CAN OCCUR.
(8) Do these steps to remove the fuel tube [16] from the hose [15] and from the fuel pump [1]:
(a) Use two wrenches to disconnect the fuel tube [16] from the hose [15].
(b) Remove the nut [19], bolt [20], and clamp [21] that hold the fuel tube [16] to the bottom of
the fuel pump [1].
(c) Remove the four bolts [18] that hold the fuel tube [16] and the gasket [17] to the fuel
pump [1].
(d) Remove the fuel tube [16].
1) If the gasket [17] is serviceable, then keep it with the tube for the subsequent
installation (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
(e) Install the protective covers on the fuel tube [16], hose [15], and fuel pump [1].
SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-007-F00
(9) Do these steps to disconnect the fuel supply hose [9] from the fuel pump inlet tube [12]:
(a) Remove the four bolts [11] to disconnect the fuel supply hose [9] and the gasket [10] from
the fuel pump inlet tube [12].
(b) Move the fuel supply hose [9] away from the fuel pump inlet tube [12].
NOTE: If you have difficulty when you try to move the fuel supply hose, then remove the
bolts and clamps that hold the fuel supply hose to the fan case.
1) If the gasket [10] is serviceable, then keep it with the tube for the subsequent
installation (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
2) Install the protective covers on the fuel supply hose [9] and the fuel pump inlet
tube [12].
SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-008-F00
(10) Do these steps to remove the fuel pump [1]:
73-11-11
(a) Make sure that the protective covers are installed on all openings in the fuel pump [1].
(b) Remove the QAD bolt [22] and retaining packing [28].
1) Install it in the opposite position with the jacking block, SPL-2189.
a) Keep the ball seat washer [23] for the subsequent installation.
WARNING
BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU REMOVE THE FUEL PUMP . THE FUEL PUMP
WEIGHS APPROXIMATELY 46.3 POUNDS (21 KG). THE WEIGHT CAN
CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT .
CAUTION
DO NOT LIFT THE FUEL PUMP BY THE DRIVE SHAFT . DO NOT LET THE
DRIVE SHAFT SUPPORT THE WEIGHT OF THE FUEL PUMP . IF THE
DRIVE SHAFT SUPPORTS THE WEIGHT OF THE FUEL PUMP , IT CAN
CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE FUEL PUMP SEALS.
CAUTION
YOU MUST HOLD THE WEIGHT OF THE FUEL PUMP AS YOU LOOSEN
THE QAD RING. IF YOU DO NOT CORRECTLY HOLD THE WEIGHT (ALL
OF THE TIME) AS YOU LOOSEN THE QAD RING, THE QAD RING WILL
NOT OPEN.
(c) Do these steps to loosen the QAD ring [24] (Figure 403).
1) Use a non-metallic hammer to hit the QAD ring [24] in the counterclockwise
direction.
CAUTION
DO NOT TIGHTEN THE QAD BOLT TO MORE THAN 300 POUND-
INCHES (34 NEWTON METERS). IF YOU TIGHTEN THE QAD BOLT
TOO MUCH, YOU CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE QAD RING.
2) Tighten the QAD bolt [22] to 300 in-lb (34 N·m).
3) Continue to hit the QAD ring [24] and tighten the QAD bolt [22] until the QAD
ring [24] moves to the open position.
(d) Carefully disengage the fuel pump [1] drive shaft from the Accessory Gear Box (AGB).
(e) Remove the fuel pump [1] from the engine.
1) Install the cover on the AGB mounting face.
(f) Remove and discard the packing support [25].
(g) Remove and discard the O-ring [26] from the fuel pump drive shaft.
SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-009-F00
(11) If it is necessary, do these steps to remove the fuel pump inlet tube [12] from the fuel pump [1].
(a) Remove the four bolts [14] that hold the fuel pump inlet tube [12] and gasket [13] to the
fuel pump [1].
(b) Remove the fuel pump inlet tube [12] and gasket [13].
1) If the gasket [13] is serviceable, then keep it with the tube for the subsequent
installation (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
2) Install protective covers on the fuel pump inlet tube [12] and fuel pump [1].
SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-010-F00
(12) If the fuel pump is replaced, remove the fuel filter differential pressure switch from the fuel
pump for installation on the replacement fuel pump (TASK 73-34-01-000-801-F00).
73-11-11
SUBTASK 73-11-11-620-001-F00
(13) If it is necessary to preserve the fuel pump, do this task: Fuel Pump Preservation,
TASK 73-11-01-600-801-F00.

F . Prepare for the Installation
SUBTASK 73-11-11-840-001-F00
(1) Do these steps to prepare the installation of the parts removed from the defective fuel pump:
(a) Remove the protective covers from the disconnected parts.
(b) Do these steps to examine the parts:
1) Clean the mating surfaces of the parts and the adjacent areas.
2) Examine the mating surfaces of the parts and the adjacent areas.
(c) Install the protective covers on the disconnected parts.
(d) Examine the gaskets to make sure that they are serviceable
(TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
(e) Make sure that the drain plug [2] is correctly installed.
1) If it not installed or is loose, install the drain plug [2].
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB
POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR
SKIN.
a) Lubricate the new O-ring [3] with oil, D00623 [CP5066], or vaseline, D00672
[CP5070].
b) Install the O-ring [3] on the drain plug [2].
c) Lubricate the threads of the drain plug [2] with oil, D00623 [CP5066], or
vaseline, D00672 [CP5070].
d) Install the drain plug [2].
e) Tighten the drain plug [2] to 45.0 in-lb (5.08 N·m) - 55.0 in-lb (6.21 N·m).
73-11-11
f) Install safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on
the drain plug [2].
SUBTASK 73-11-11-420-001-F00
(2) If you install a replacement pump, install the fuel filter differential pressure switch that follows:
(a) Do this task: Fuel Filter Differential Pressure Switch or Fuel Filter Clogging Indicator
Installation, TASK 73-34-01-400-801-F00.
G. Fuel Pump Installation
SUBTASK 73-11-11-420-002-F00
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU INSTALL THE FUEL PUMP INLET-TUBE IN THE
CORRECT ORIENTATION. THE FUEL SUPPLY TUBE MUST DESCRIBE AN
"S" SHAPE WHEN YOU SEE IT FROM THE LEFT OF THE ENGINE.
INSTALLING THE TUBE IN A DIFFERENT POSITION MAY RESULT IN
CHAFING OF THE TUBE WITH THE AGB LINK AND CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE
FUEL PUMP INLET TUBE.
(1) Do these steps to install the fuel pump inlet tube [12] on the fuel pump [1].
(a) Lubricate these parts:
1) Lubricate the new gasket [13] with vaseline, D00672 [CP5070], or oil, D00623
[CP5066].
2) Lubricate the threads of the bolts [14] with graphite mineral vaseline, D00601
[CP2101].
(b) Remove the protective covers from the bottom of the fuel pump inlet tube [12] and
applicable covers from the fuel pump [1].
(c) Install the bolts [14] to connect the fuel pump inlet tube [12] and gasket [13] to the fuel
pump [1].
1) Tighten the bolts [14] to 97 in-lb (11.0 N·m) - 110 in-lb (12.4 N·m).
SUBTASK 73-11-11-480-001-F00
(2) Do these steps to prepare for the installation of the fuel pump [1].
WARNING
DO NOT GET FUEL IN YOUR MOUTH, OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN.
DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE FUEL. KEEP THE FUEL
AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . FUEL IS POISONOUS AND
FLAMMABLE, WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS OR
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Lubricate a new O-ring [26] with vaseline, D00672 [CP5070], or oil, D00623 [CP5066].
(b) Install the O-ring [26] in the groove of the fuel pump drive shaft.
(c) Lubricate a new packing support [25] with vaseline, D00672 [CP5070], or oil, D00623
[CP5066].
(d) Install the new packing [27] on the packing support [25].
(e) Install the packing support [25] on the fixed part of the QAD ring [24].
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB
POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN.
(f) Lubricate the threads of the QAD bolt [22] with engine oil, D00599 [CP2442].
73-11-11
SUBTASK 73-11-11-420-003-F00
(3) Do these steps to install the fuel pump [1]:
(a) If it is necessary, turn the Accessory Gear Box (AGB) output shaft to align with the fuel
pump drive shaft (TASK 72-63-01-000-801-F00).
1) Turn the handcranking drive pad with a 3/4-inch square drive to align the AGB drive
recess with the fuel pump drive shaft splines.
WARNING
BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU INSTALL THE FUEL PUMP . THE FUEL PUMP
WEIGHS APPROXIMATELY 46.3 POUNDS (21 KG). THE WEIGHT CAN
CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT .
CAUTION
DO NOT LIFT THE FUEL PUMP BY THE DRIVE SHAFT . DO NOT LET THE
DRIVE SHAFT SUPPORT THE WEIGHT OF THE FUEL PUMP . IF THE
DRIVE SHAFT SUPPORTS THE WEIGHT OF THE FUEL PUMP , IT CAN
CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE FUEL PUMP SEALS.
(b) Engage the fuel pump [1] in the AGB.
(c) Turn the QAD ring [24] clockwise until it engages with the fuel pump flange.
(d) Make sure that the ball seat washer [23] is correctly installed on the QAD ring [24].
(e) Install the QAD bolt [22] and retaining packing [28] in the QAD ring [24].
CAUTION
DO NOT TIGHTEN THE QAD BOLT TO MORE THAN 327 IN-LB (37 N.M).
IF YOU TIGHTEN THE QAD BOLT TOO MUCH, YOU CAN CAUSE
DAMAGE TO THE QAD RING.
(f) Tighten the QAD bolt [22] to 195 in-lb (22 N·m) - 230 in-lb (26 N·m).
1) Lightly hit all around the outer surface of the QAD ring [24] with a non-metallic
hammer or a rubber mallet.
2) Loosen the QAD bolt [22].
(g) Tighten the QAD bolt [22] to 292 in-lb (33 N·m) - 327 in-lb (37 N·m).
1) Lightly hit all around the outer surface of the QAD ring [24] with a non-metallic
hammer or a rubber mallet.
(h) Tighten the QAD bolt [22] to 292 in-lb (33 N·m) - 327 in-lb (37 N·m).
(i) Measure the clearance between the QAD ring [24] and flange of the QAD ring [24]
support.
1) If the clearance is more than 1.0 in. (25.4 mm) or less than 0.25 in. (6.4 mm),
replace the QAD ring [24].
(j) Install safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on the QAD
bolt [22].
NOTE: The final torque of the QAD bolt [22] of the QAD ring [24] will be done at the end
of this procedure after the engine test.
(k) Attach to the QAD bolt [22] an indicator that mentions the need to finalize the tightening
procedure after the engine tests.
(l) Do this task: Handcranking Drive Cover Installation, TASK 72-63-01-400-801-F00.
73-11-11
SUBTASK 73-11-11-420-004-F00
(4) Do these steps to connect the fuel supply hose [9] to the fuel pump inlet tube [12] on the fuel
pump [1].
(a) Lubricate these parts:
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB
POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN.
1) Lubricate the gasket [10] with oil, D00623 [CP5066], or vaseline, D00672 [CP5070].
2) Lubricate the threads of the bolts [11] with Pure Nickel Special compound, D00006.
(b) Remove the protective covers from the fuel supply hose [9] and top end of the fuel pump
inlet tube [12].
(c) Put the fuel supply hose [9] and gasket [10] in the correct position on the fuel pump inlet
tube [12].
NOTE: If it is not easy to put the fuel supply hose into its position, remove the bolts and
clamps that hold the fuel supply hose to the fan case.
(d) Use the four bolts [11] to connect the fuel supply hose [9] and gasket [10] to the fuel
pump inlet tube [12].
1) Tighten the bolts [11] to 50 in-lb (5.6 N·m) - 55 in-lb (6.2 N·m).
(e) If you removed or loosened the clamps, install the clamps and bolts to connect the fuel
supply hose [9] to the fan case.
1) Tighten the bolts for the clamps to 98 in-lb (11.1 N·m) - 110 in-lb (12.4 N·m).
a) Make sure that there is a clearance of 0.625 in. (15.9 mm) between fuel supply
hose [9] and adjacent electrical wiring.
SUBTASK 73-11-11-420-005-F00
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO TIGHTEN THE TUBE COUPLING NUTS. USE ONE
TO HOLD THE MATING PART , AND THE OTHER TO TIGHTEN THE
COUPLING NUT . IF YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, DAMAGE TO THE
TUBES AND MATING PART CAN OCCUR.
(5) Do these steps to install the fuel tube [16] on the fuel pump [1] and on the hose [15].
(a) Remove the protective covers from the hose [15], fuel tube [16], and applicable covers
from the fuel pump [1].
(b) Lubricate these parts:
1) Lubricate the gasket [17] with vaseline, D00672 [CP5070], or oil, D00623 [CP5066].
2) Lubricate the threads of the bolts [18] and bolt [20] with graphite mineral vaseline,
D00601 [CP2101].
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB
POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN.
3) Lubricate the threads of the nipple of the fuel tube [16] with oil, D00623 [CP5066],
or vaseline, D00672 [CP5070].
73-11-11
(c) Put the fuel tube [16] with the new gasket [17] in the correct position between the fuel
pump [1] and hose [15].
(d) Install the four bolts [18] that hold the fuel tube [16] and gasket [17] to the fuel pump [1].
(e) Connect the fuel tube [16] to the hose [15].
(f) Use the nut [19], bolt [20], and clamp [21] to connect the fuel tube [16] to the bottom of
the fuel pump [1].
(g) Make sure that the fuel tube [16] does not touch other parts of the engine.
(h) Tighten the bolts [18] to 49 in-lb (5.5 N·m) - 53 in-lb (6.0 N·m).
(i) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut between fuel tube [16] and hose [15] to
900 in-lb (102 N·m) - 1100 in-lb (124 N·m).
(j) Tighten the nut [19] to 62 in-lb (7 N·m) - 71 in-lb (8 N·m).
SUBTASK 73-11-11-410-001-F00
(6) Do this task: Main Oil/Fuel Heat Exchanger Installation, TASK 79-21-02-400-801-F00.
NOTE: Do not tighten the nut [6] and bolt [7] that hold the fuel tube [8] to the bracket before
the connection of the fuel tube [8] to the hose [4].
SUBTASK 73-11-11-420-006-F00
(7) Connect the fuel tube [8] to the hose [4].
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO TIGHTEN THE TUBE COUPLING NUTS. USE
ONE TO HOLD THE MATING PART , AND THE OTHER TO TIGHTEN THE
COUPLING NUT . IF YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, DAMAGE TO
THE TUBES AND MATING PART CAN OCCUR.
(a) Remove the protective covers from fuel tube [8] and hose [4].
WARNING
DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB
POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN.
(b) Lubricate the threads of the nipple of the hose [4] with oil, D00623 [CP5066], or vaseline,
D00672 [CP5070].
(c) Use your hands to connect the fuel tube [8] to the hose [4].
(d) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut, between fuel tube [8] and hose [4] to a
torque of 900 in-lb (102 N·m) to 1100 in-lb (124 N·m).
(e) Tighten the nut [6] that holds the clamp [5] to 62 in-lb (7 N·m) - 71 in-lb (8 N·m).

CAUTION

BRIGHT . IF YOU DO NOT OBEY , DAMAGE TO THE ENGINE CAN OCCUR.
CAUTION

THE P5 OVERHEAD PANEL ARE OFF . IF YOU DRY-MOTOR THE ENGINE
AND APPLICABLE LOW PRESSURE LIGHTS ARE ON, DAMAGE TO THE
ENGINE CAN OCCUR.
CAUTION

THERE IS FUEL PRESSURE TO THE FUEL PUMP INLET . THE FUEL PUMP
AND HYDRO MECHANICAL UNIT ARE FUEL LUBRICATED. ZERO FUEL
PRESSURE CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE FUEL PUMP AND HYDRO
MECHANICAL UNIT .

J. Final Torque of the QAD Ring
SUBTASK 73-11-11-420-007-F00
CAUTION
DO NOT TIGHTEN THE QAD BOLT TO MORE THAN 327 IN-LB (37 N.M). IF
YOU TIGHTEN THE QAD BOLT TOO MUCH, YOU CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO
THE QAD RING.
(1) Torque the QAD bolt [22] of the QAD ring [24].
(a) Remove safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], and indicator attached to the QAD bolt [22].
(b) Tighten the QAD bolt [22] to 292 in-lb (33 N·m) - 327 in-lb (37 N·m).
(c) Loosen the QAD bolt [22].
(d) Tighten the QAD bolt [22] to 195 in-lb (22 N·m) - 230 in-lb (26 N·m).
(e) Install safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on the QAD
bolt [22].

G. Removal of Minor Surface Indications from the Fuel Filter Cover and Gear Housing Assembly

PARTICLES TOUCH YOU. THE PARTICLES FROM BLENDING CAN CAUSE
DAMAGE, INJURY , OR IRRITATION TO YOU. USE PROTECTION

APPROVED RESPIRATOR.
(1) Hand blend to remove the surface indications from the repair surface
(TASK 70-10-07-800-801-F00), (Figure 803).
(a) The table below gives dimensions for a hand blending.
73-11-11
Table 804/73-11-11-993-804-F00

(b) Only abrasive consumables of silicon carbide grit or corundum (alumina) are permitted.
(c) For hand blending use a abrasive cloth, G00363, or emery cloth, G00744, unless the
steps specify differently.
(d) Make sure that the Dimension P (blend depth) is approximately 1.25 times the Dimension
A (indication depth).
(e) Make sure that the blend length is a minimum of 10 times the Dimension P (blend depth)
in all directions.
1) If it is possible, larger length-to-depth ratios are recommended.
(f) Use a 10x hand held magnifying lens, STD-1070, to make sure that the blend shape is
smooth, continuous, and round-bottomed, extending as far as necessary from the initially
deviated area.
(g) Blend the indications that have a radial direction in the same (radial) direction.
(h) If the blended part involves a radius, make the radius the specified shape (Figure 803).
1) If the radius is not specified, make it a shape almost the same as the original
contour.
2) If it is necessary to find the original radius, refer to the location of the part with no
indication or an equivalent part.
(i) It is permitted to blend sharp edges with abrasive tools.
1) Coarse grades of abrasives or files can be used for fast metal removal.
2) Make sure to give a part a smooth surface finish with fine grades of abrasives or
abrasive cloth, G00363.
(j) To keep abrasives working properly, do not let the abrasive become loaded up with
removed material.
(k) Start the blend with coarser grades of abrasive files for fast initial removal of the
indication but not coarser than necessary to remove the indication.
(l) Finish the blend with the finer grades of abrasive files for fast initial removal of the
indication but not coarser than necessary to remove the indication.
SUBTASK 73-11-11-211-002-F00
(2) Visually examine the repaired area with a 10x hand held magnifying lens, STD-1070.
(a) No cracks permitted.
SUBTASK 73-11-11-230-001-F00
(3) Do a fluorescent penetrant inspection, sensibility level 2, of the blended areas
(TASK 70-40-01-230-801-F00 or TASK 70-40-01-910-801-F00).
73-11-11
(a) Indications less than 0.0118 in. (0.30 mm) are non-interpretable.
(b) Indications more than 0.0118 in. (0.30 mm) are not permitted.
(c) Linear indications are not permitted.
NOTE: Linear indications are indications that are at minimum four times longer than they
are wide.
SUBTASK 73-11-11-300-001-F00
(4) Wet the repaired area or bare surfaces that to be touched up with de-ionized water, G02418.
NOTE: Surface must stay wet between surface preparation and solution application.

73-21-00-740-803-F00EEC BITE TEST - RECENT FAULTSPDF p. 303
Open original AMM PDF - page 303 ↗ 
2. EEC BITE TEST - RECENT FAULTS
(Figure 501)
NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.
A. General
(1) Use the Flight Management Computer/Control Display Unit (FMCS CDU) to do the Engine
EEC BITE TEST .
(2) The EEC BITE - Recent Faults procedure shows maintenance messages for the three most
recent flight legs.
(a) The maintenance messages are stored in the EEC and show on the FMCS CDU.
(b) The FMCS CDU shows the maintenance messages for only one engine at a time.
(3) The FMCS CDU shows only one maintenance message on each screen.
(a) The FMCS CDU shows the page you are on and the total number of pages.
NOTE: If the FMCS CDU screen shows 2/4, you are on page 2 of 4 pages.
(4) There are five categories of maintenance messages. The time limited dispatch limits which are
given below are for on-condition events.
NOTE: The CFM56-7B Engine Shop Manual (CFMI-TP .SM.10), ATA 05-17-01 is the certified
authority for the Time Limited Dispatch.
(a) ENGINE CONTROL LIGHT Faults - You can not dispatch the airplane with this fault.
NOTE: These faults cause the ENGINE CONTROL Light to come ON.
(b) ALTERNATE MODE LIGHT Fault - Refer to the Minimum Equipment List (MEL) for the
dispatch limits.
NOTE: These faults cause the ALTN Mode Light to come ON.
73-21-00
(c) SHORT TIME Fault - Calculate the remaining flight hours that you can operate with this
fault as follows:
1) The remaining Flight Hours (R) = 150 flight hours - "Q", where "Q" is the scheduled
maintenance interval your airline uses to check the EEC BITE TEST - RECENT
FAULTS, SHORT TIME category.
NOTE: If your airline looks for EEC faults every 70 flight hour, then "Q" = 70. If your
airline looks for EEC faults every 150 flight hours, then "Q" = 150.
(d) LONG TIME Fault - Calculate the remaining flight hours that you can operate with this
fault as follows:
1) The remaining Flight Hours (T) = 500 flight hours - "S/2", where "S/2" is one half of
the scheduled maintenance interval your airline uses to check the EEC BITE TEST -
RECENT FAULTS, LONG TIME category.
NOTE: If your airline looks for EEC faults every 70 flight hour, then "S/2" = 35. If
your airline looks for EEC faults every 150 flight hours, then "S/2" = 75.
(e) ECONOMIC Awareness Fault - There are no time limits for dispatch. Repair the problem
at a convenient time.
(5) The recent faults function will show the maintenance message numbers for the most recent
three flight legs and one ground operation.
(a) Flight Legs 1 through 3 are the three most recent flight legs.
(b) Flight Leg 0:
1) Can show maintenance messages that occur more than 30 seconds after landing
from the last flight leg.
2) Can show the most recent ground run of the engine.
NOTE: If the engine is started and stopped more than once between flights, Flight
Leg 0 will contain data from the last ground run of the engine.
(c) The X below the flight leg number indicates that the fault occurred on that flight leg.
1) For flight legs that did not have the fault, the space below the flight legs number is
blank.
B. References
Reference Title
24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
D. Procedure
SUBTASK 73-21-00-740-003-F00
(1) Do these steps to get the RECENT FAULTS data for Engine 1 or Engine 2:
(a) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811.
(b) Get access to the FMCS CDU in the flight compartment.
(c) Press the INIT REF key to show the PERF INIT screen on the FMCS CDU.
NOTE: The FMCS CDU does not support a type-ahead function. You must have the
prompt on the FMCS CDU screen before you type in the response.
73-21-00
(d) Push these line select keys (LSK) on the FMCS CDU:
1) INDEX
2) MAINT
NOTE: This LSK causes the MAINT BITE INDEX screen to show.
3) ENGINE
NOTE: This LSK causes the ENGINE/EXCEED BITE INDEX screen to show.
4) Applicable ENGINE X, (X = 1 or 2)
NOTE: This LSK causes the ENGINE X BITE TEST MAIN MENU to show. Also, the
ENGINE X LSK automatically applies power to the EEC and causes the
EEC to initialize. The FMCS CDU will show INITIALIZING EEC X and EEC
SORTING FAULT HISTORY DATA for a short time, just before the ENGINE
X BITE TEST MAIN MENU shows.
(e) Push the RECENT FAULTS LSK.
NOTE: This LSK causes the RECENT FAULTS screen to show. The HISTORY LSK, on
the Recent Faults screen, will show the fault history for the maintenance
message that shows on the screen. The NEXT PAGE key will continue to show
the other faults in the RECENT FAULTS format. The INDEX LSK will send you to
the ENGINE X BITE TEST MAIN MENU.
1) If the FOR CH A ONLY or FOR CH B ONLY screen shows, then, do this task: CH A
(B) EEC DATA NOT AVAILABLE - Fault Isolation, FIM 73-05 TASK 803.
2) Record this data from each screen:
a) The Dispatch Level
NOTE: The dispatch level will show at the top of the screen. The FMCS CDU
will display the faults in the order of their dispatch level. The ENGINE
CONTROL light faults will show first, then the ALTERNATE MODE
LIGHT faults, then the SHORT TIME faults, then the LONG TIME
faults, and last the ECONOMIC awareness faults. Refer to the
CFM56-7B Engine Shop Manual 05-17-01, or the General Statement
of this procedure for the Time Limited Dispatch capabilities for each
category of message.
b) Maintenance Message Number
NOTE: A seven digit number with this format: AA - XDDDN. AA = ATA
Chapter, X = EEC Channel (1=Channel A, 2=Channel B, 3=Channels
A and B), DDD = a unique fault number, and N = Engine Position
(1=Engine 1, 2=Engine 2). If the message is reported with an engine
position equal to zero, then for the applicable engine, do the corrective
action for Engine Position Signal is out of Range (73-X138N)
(FIM 73-22 TASK 806).
3) Push the NEXT PAGE key to see the subsequent maintenance message.
a) Continue to push the NEXT PAGE key until you record all of the faults.
4) If you want to go back to the previous message, push the PREV PAGE key.
5) If the ENGINE CONTROL light was ON and none of the ENGINE CONTROL light
messages show during the EEC BITE Test, then, do this task: EEC TEST ,
TASK 73-21-00-700-804-F00.
a) Look for one or more of these Maintenance Message:
73-21-00
b) 73-10201, 73-10202, 73-20201 73-20202, 73-30201, 73-30202, 73-10211,
73-10212, 73-20211 73-20212, 73-30211, 73-30212, 73-10221, 73-10222,
73-20221 73-20222, 73-30221 or 73-30222.
NOTE: These INTERNAL EEC messages can set the ENGINE CONTROL
light, but the problem that causes the fault also causes problems with
the EEC BITE Test. When this occurs, the EEC cannot write to the
EEC fault memory.
c) Do the corrective action in the FIM for the messages that you find.
(f) If the fault data is not available from one of the two channels (A and B) of the EEC, the
screen will show the EEC channel that has data.
1) Example:
FOR CH B (A) ONLY , CH A (B) EEC DATA NOT AVAILABLE, CAN NOT ACCESS
CH A (B)
(g) If you are in RECENT FAULTS and there are no faults stored for the flight legs 0 through
3, the screen will show NO RECENT FAULTS STORED.
(h) If you wish to do other tests, push the INDEX LSK several times, until the correct menu
shows.
(i) To end the test, push the INIT REF key.
NOTE: This causes the test to stop and automatically removes electrical power from the
EEC.

5. FADEC System Test
(Figure 501)
A. General
(1) This task is the complete test of the FADEC (Full Authority Digital Engine Controller) system.
(2) The task is used when the EEC or the Engine is replaced.
B. References
Reference Title
71-00-00-700-807-F00 Test 12 - Actuators Test (P/B 501)
73-21-60-470-801-F00 EEC Software Load (P/B 201)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
73-21-00
D. FADEC System Test
SUBTASK 73-21-00-700-002-F00
(1) When you do the FADEC system test, you must make sure the configuration of the engine and
EEC is compatible with the opposite engine and EEC, the airplane and thrust reversers.
NOTE: Refer to Boeing SB 737-71-1588 for concurrent requirements necessary for installation
of a T ech Insertion (TI) CFM56-7BXX/3 engine or CFM56-7BXX SAC engine
incorporating CFM-SB 72-0583 on 737-600/700/800/900/BBJ airplanes from Line
Positions 1 through 2229.
NOTE: Refer to Boeing SB 737-71-1551 for concurrent requirements necessary for installation
of TI CFM56-7BXX/3 engine or CFM56-7BXX SAC engine incorporating CFM-SB
72-0583 on 737-700/800 airplanes from Line Positions 2230 through 2252.
NOTE: Refer to Boeing SB 737-71-1582 for concurrent requirements necessary for installation
of non-TI engine (CFM56-7BXX) engine or CFM56-7BXX SAC engines incorporating
CFM-SB 72-0583 on airplanes delivered with TI engines (CFM56-7BXX/3) on
737-600/700/800/900/BBJ airplanes from Line Positions 2253 and on and all
737-900ER airplanes.
NOTE: A T ech Insertion (TI) engine has a “/3” after the thrust rating on the engine data plate.
An example of a Singular Annular Combustor (SAC) Engine with TI is CFM56-7B22/3.
An example of a non-TI engine is CFM56-7B22. The “XX” in CFM56-7BXX/3 is the
thrust rating of the engine.
(a) Make sure the EEC software version on each engine is compatible with the other engine,
thrust reverser and airplane. Refer to the task EEC Software Load,
TASK 73-21-60-470-801-F00.
1) Intermix of some EEC software versions is not permitted.
2) Some EEC software versions must be installed on both engines at the same time.
3) Some EEC software versions require concurrent changes to the airplane
configuration.
SUBTASK 73-21-00-740-004-F00
(2) For an EEC replacement, do this task: Erase All EEC Faults, TASK 73-21-00-800-801-F00
SUBTASK 73-21-00-730-001-F00
(3) Do these steps to do the FADEC System Test:
(a) Do this task: Prepare for the Tests, TASK 73-21-00-840-801-F00.
(b) Do this task: EEC TEST , TASK 73-21-00-700-804-F00.
(c) Do this task: IDENT/CONFIG, TASK 73-21-00-700-808-F00.
(d) Do this task: Test 12 - Actuators Test, TASK 71-00-00-700-807-F00.

73-21-00-700-804-F00EEC TESTPDF p. 315

6. EEC TEST
(Figure 501)
A. General
(1) The EEC Test causes each channel of the EEC to do an internal EEC test.
(2) To make sure that the technician can see the correct operation of the EEC, the test causes
these fight deck effects:
(a) ENGINE CONTROL light
NOTE: This light is found on the Engine Module in the P5 Aft Overhead Panel.
(b) ALTN light
NOTE: This light is found on the EEC MODE Switch in the P5 Aft Overhead Panel.
(c) Fuel FILTER BYPASS light
NOTE: This light is found on the Fuel Control Module in the P5 Fwd Overhead Panel.
(d) OIL FILTER BYPASS message
NOTE: This message is found on the Engine Display.
(3) If faults are found during the test, the FMCS CDU will show the faults at the end of the test.
B. Location Zones
Zone Area
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
C. Procedure
SUBTASK 73-21-00-730-002-F00
(1) Do these steps to do the EEC Test:
(a) If not already done, do this task: Prepare for the Tests, TASK 73-21-00-840-801-F00.
(b) If the FMCS CDU is not active from other EEC tests, do these steps:
1) Get access to the FMCS CDU in the flight compartment.
2) Press the INIT REF key to show the PERF INIT screen on the FMCS CDU.
NOTE: The FMCS CDU does not support a type-ahead function. You must have
the prompt on the FMCS CDU screen before you type in the response.
3) Push these line select keys (LSK) on the FMCS CDU:
a) INDEX
b) MAINT
NOTE: This LSK causes the MAINT BITE INDEX screen to show.
4) Push these line select keys (LSK) on the FMCS CDU:
a) ENGINE
NOTE: This LSK causes the ENGINE/EXCEED BITE INDEX screen to show.
73-21-00
b) Applicable ENGINE X, (X = 1 or 2)
NOTE: This LSK causes the ENGINE X BITE TEST MAIN MENU to show.
Also, the ENGINE X LSK automatically applies power to the EEC and
causes the EEC to initialize. The FMCS CDU will show INITIALIZING
EEC X and EEC SORTING FAULT HISTORY DATA for a short time,
just before the ENGINE X BITE TEST MAIN MENU shows.
c) GROUND TESTS
NOTE: This LSK causes the ENGINE X BITE TEST GROUND TEST menu to
show.
d) If the FOR CH A ONLY or FOR CH B ONLY screen shows, then, do this task:
CH A (B) EEC DATA NOT AVAILABLE - Fault Isolation, FIM 73-05 TASK 803.
e) EEC TEST
f) START TEST .
NOTE: You can stop the test at this time if you push the ABORT LSK. The
screen will show the test is not completed because ABORT was
selected.
(c) If the FMCS CDU is active from other EEC tests, do this step:
1) Push the INDEX LSK to get access to the ENGINE X BITE TEST GROUND TEST
MENU.
(d) Make sure that these indications are off:
1) The ENG CONTROL light
NOTE: This light is found on the Engine Module in the P5 Aft Overhead Panel.
2) The ALTN light
NOTE: This light is found on the EEC MODE Switch in the P5 Aft Overhead Panel.
3) The fuel FILTER BYPASS light
NOTE: This light is found on the Fuel Control Module in the P5 Fwd Overhead
Panel.
4) OIL FILTER BYPASS message.
NOTE: This message is found on the Engine Display.
(e) Push the CONTINUE LSK and the test screen tells you CH A Test Is In Progress.
NOTE: The test should take less than one minute.
(f) Make sure that these indications are on for EEC channel A:
1) The ENG CONTROL light
2) The ALTN light
3) The fuel FILTER BYPASS light
4) The OIL FILTER BYPASS message.
(g) Push the CONTINUE LSK.
(h) Make sure that these indications are off:
1) The ENG CONTROL light
2) The ALTN light
3) The fuel FILTER BYPASS light
4) The OIL FILTER BYPASS message.
73-21-00
(i) Push the CONTINUE LSK and the test screen tells you CH B Test Is In Progress.
NOTE: The test should take less than one minute.
(j) Make sure that these indications are on for EEC channel B:
1) The ENG CONTROL light
2) The ALTN light
3) The fuel FILTER BYPASS light
4) The OIL FILTER BYPASS message.
(k) Push the CONTINUE LSK.
(l) Make sure that these indications are off:
1) The ENG CONTROL light
2) The ALTN light
3) The fuel FILTER BYPASS light
4) The OIL FILTER BYPASS message.
(m) If all of the indications above do not agree with the expected value (light was on when
expected off or light was off when expected on), the test is not valid.
1) You must correct the problem with the applicable indication and do the test again.
NOTE: Refer to the Observed Fault Index for the applicable ATA (Chapter 73 or
Chapter 79).
(n) Push the TEST RESULTS LSK.
(o) If faults are found, the test screen will show EEC TEST FAILED and you will find the
maintenance message number (MSG NBR) and a short description of the fault.
NOTE: If there is more than one fault, the page you are on and the total number of
pages will show on the screen. Example: 2/5 shows you are on page 2 of 5
pages.
1) Push the NEXT PAGE LSK until you have recorded all of the maintenance
messages that show.
2) Do the corrective action in the FIM for the messages that show.
(p) If there are no faults, then the test screen will show EEC TEST PASSED.
(q) Push the END TEST LSK
NOTE: This causes TEST COMPLETED screen to show on the FMCS CDU.
(r) If you wish to do other tests, push the INDEX LSK several times, until the correct menu
shows.

7. T/R LEVER INTLK (Interlock) TEST
(Figure 501)
A. General
(1) The T/R Lever Interlock test makes sure that each channel of the EEC can enable and disable
the thrust reverser interlock.
(2) If a problem is found during the test, the FMCS CDU will show the applicable faults at the end
of the test.
73-21-00

NOTE: The test can be stopped if you push the ABORT LSK. The screen will show the

(h) Do the thrust reverser lever interlock test:
1) Type "OK" on the FMCS CDU pad when you are ready to start the test.
2) Push the CONTINUE LSK.
NOTE: This starts the check for Channel A.
3) Follow the instructions on the FMCS CDU test screen:
a) Move the applicable reverse thrust lever rearward to the reverse idle
mechanical stop.
b) Stop (wait) for a minimum of five seconds.
NOTE: The first channel of the EEC will make sure that the TRA signal from
the thrust lever angle resolver does not command a thrust value that is
higher than reverse idle thrust. The test may not show the
maintenance messages correctly if you stop for less than five
seconds.
c) Push the CONTINUE LSK.
73-21-00
d) Move the reverse thrust lever rearward to the full reverse thrust position.
<1> If you cannot move the reverse thrust lever, make sure you stop for at
least 5 seconds in the subsequent step to set the maintenance
messages.
e) Stop (wait) for a minimum of five seconds.
NOTE: The first channel of the EEC will make sure that the TRA signal from
the thrust lever angle resolver commands a thrust value that is
maximum reverse thrust. The test may not show the maintenance
messages correctly if you stop for less than five seconds.
f) Push the CONTINUE LSK.
g) Move the reverse thrust lever forward to the retracted (stow) position.
h) Push the CONTINUE LSK.
NOTE: This starts the check for Channel B.
i) Move the reverse thrust lever rearward to the reverse idle mechanical stop.
j) Stop (wait) for a minimum of five seconds.
NOTE: The second channel of the EEC will make sure that the TRA signal
from the thrust lever angle resolver does not command a thrust value
that is higher than reverse idle thrust. The test may not show the
maintenance messages correctly if you stop for less than five
seconds.
k) Push the CONTINUE LSK.
l) Move the reverse thrust lever rearward to the full reverse thrust position.
<1> If you cannot move the reverse thrust lever, make sure that you stop for
at least 5 seconds in the subsequent step to set the maintenance
messages.
m) Stop (wait) for a minimum of five seconds.
NOTE: The second channel of the EEC will make sure that the TRA signal
from the thrust lever angle resolver commands a thrust value that is
maximum reverse thrust. The test may not show the maintenance
messages correctly if you stop for less than five seconds.
n) Push the CONTINUE LSK.
o) Move the reverse thrust lever forward to the retracted (stow) position.
4) Push the TEST RESULTS LSK.
a) Look for the T/R LEVER INTLK TEST PASSED screen.
(i) If faults are found, the test screen will show T/R LEVER INTLK TEST FAILED and you

fault.
